The `>3` semantics genuinely reuse `_pit._sane`, the threshold is read at call time with no env override anywhere in the cron path, and there is no look-ahead — the quarterly row is always filtered to <= the decision date. The report's honesty is above average: it discloses its own two broken assertions and the one mutant that failed to kill. The single real weakness is coverage, not correctness: no test exercises run(), and I killed that claim concretely by deleting the entire layer-4c block from run() and separately disabling the Discord line — both mutants survive 178/178, and today's empty `mag` makes the A/B run blind to them too. So 'mutation 6/6 giết' promises more than it has. I closed that hole by hand — forcing one synthetic warn through the real run() on live BQ produced rc=0, all five snapshot fields, and a correctly rendered 📏 line with the held ticker split out — which is why this is CONFIRMED rather than INCONCLUSIVE. One real defect: the finding miscounts its own evidence — it says three logs died before running, but only two did; the third completed successfully under the same sandbox doing purely local work, which is actually a free negative control proving the block is network-specific. That wrong sentence has already propagated into the canonical incident file and should be corrected there. Finally, the finding's flagged-as-unconfirmed secondary hypothesis has since been resolved by the user: they ran codex by hand because claude died at market open, so there is no provider-gate bypass, and recommended option B targets a hole that does not exist — leaving A versus C as the only live policy choice."}

The E3 rationale was the real attack surface since no script was committed for it, so I rebuilt the entire weekday measurement from the cache: every headline ratio reproduced within noise, and the two attacks most likely to kill it both failed — the Monday effect is STRONGER out of sample (1.439 in 2020-26 vs 1.254 in 2016-19) and survives with the episode-merge rule removed entirely, so it is neither IS-overfit nor a merging artifact. No look-ahead: no forward column is read, and the effect is stronger under the production liquidity gate than on the raw panel, so it is not an illiquidity artifact. Two defects, neither voiding the claim: the live Monday shell script still quotes 1,408x / p=1,6e-37 / 1,114x, a trio that reproduces from no committed state (only the .md was synced); and the headline 1,409x is a post-2020 phenomenon that is statistically absent in 2016-2019 (1.202x, p=0.14) — a caveat missing from an otherwise exemplary limitations section, though the direction is recency-favourable and the Friday -3,10% that carries the operational argument is sign-stable in every sub-period. Main's 6952ed0 caught and fixed it separately, so the gap is closed operationally, but the finding's own evidence section is what led it astray. Second, the cited hash 717303b does not exist (real: 717307f). CONFIRMED at medium confidence: every affirmative claim reproduced, but the completeness claim and one evidentiary inference did not."}
